### Proposed categories

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Proposed categories")]

The Board of Governors meets each year and considers new award categories. To date, the following categories have been proposed:

- Best Casting: rejected in 1999;[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-mentalfloss-113) will be implemented for the 2026 ceremony[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-deadline2024-110)
- [Best Popular Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Outstanding_Achievement_in_Popular_Film "Academy Award for Outstanding Achievement in Popular Film"): proposed in 2018 for presentation at the 2019 ceremony; postponed until the 2020 ceremony at the earliest (yet to be implemented)[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-114)
- Best [Stunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stunt "Stunt") Coordination: rejected every year from 1991 to 2012[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-115)[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-116)[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-117)[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-118)[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-119)
- Best Title Design: rejected in 1999[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-mentalfloss-113)

## Special categories

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Special categories")]

The Special Academy Awards are voted on by special committees, rather than by the Academy membership as a whole. They are not always presented on an annual basis.

### Current special categories

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Current special categories")]

- [Academy Honorary Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Honorary_Award "Academy Honorary Award"): since 1929
- [Academy Scientific and Technical Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Scientific_and_Technical_Award "Academy Scientific and Technical Award") (three different awards): since 1931
- [Gordon E. Sawyer Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon_E._Sawyer_Award "Gordon E. Sawyer Award"): since 1981
- [Jean Hersholt Humanitarian Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Hersholt_Humanitarian_Award "Jean Hersholt Humanitarian Award"): since 1957
- [Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving_G._Thalberg_Memorial_Award "Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award"): since 1938
- [Academy Special Achievement Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Achievement_Academy_Award "Special Achievement Academy Award"): from 1972 to 1995, and again for 2017

### Discontinued special categories

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Discontinued special categories")]

- [Academy Juvenile Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Juvenile_Award "Academy Juvenile Award"): 1934 to 1960

## Criticism and controversies

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Criticism and controversies")]

### Accusations of commercialism

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Accusations of commercialism")]

Due to the positive exposure and prestige of the Academy Awards, many studios spend around 25 million dollars and hire publicists specifically to promote their films during what is typically called the "[Oscar season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_season "Oscar season")".[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-120) This has generated accusations of the Academy Awards being influenced more by marketing and [lobbying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobbying "Lobbying") than by quality. [William Friedkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Friedkin "William Friedkin"), an Academy Award-winning film director and former producer of the ceremony, expressed this sentiment at a conference in New York in 2009, describing it as "the greatest promotion scheme that any industry ever devised for itself".[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-121)

Tim Dirks, editor of [AMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Movie_Classics "American Movie Classics")'s [Filmsite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filmsite "Filmsite"), has written of the Academy Awards:

> Unfortunately, the critical worth, artistic vision, cultural influence and innovative qualities of many films are not given the same voting weight. Especially since the 1980s, moneymaking "formula-made" blockbusters with glossy production values have often been crowd-pleasing titans (and Best Picture winners), but they haven't necessarily been great films with depth or critical acclaim by any measure.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-122)

A recent technique that has been claimed to be used during the Oscar season is the [whisper campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whisper_campaign "Whisper campaign"). These campaigns are intended to spread negative perceptions of other movies nominated and are believed to be perpetrated by those who were involved in creating the movie. Examples of whisper campaigns include the allegations against *[Zero Dark Thirty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero_Dark_Thirty "Zero Dark Thirty")* suggesting that it justifies torture and the claim that *[Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_\(film\) "Lincoln (film)")* distorts history.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-123)

### Accusations of bias

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=27 "Edit section: Accusations of bias")]

Further information: [Oscar bait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_bait "Oscar bait")

Typical criticism of the Academy Awards for Best Picture is that among the winners and nominees there is an over-representation of romantic historical epics, biographical dramas, romantic dramedies and family melodramas, most of which are released in the U.S. in the last three months of the calendar year. The Oscars have been infamously known for selecting specific genres of movies to be awarded. The term "[Oscar bait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_bait "Oscar bait")" was coined to describe such movies. This has led, at times, to more specific criticisms that the Academy is disconnected from the audience, e.g., by favoring "Oscar bait" over audience favorites or favoring historical melodramas over critically acclaimed movies that depict current life issues.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-124)

Despite the success of *[The Dark Knight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dark_Knight "The Dark Knight")*, the film did not receive a [Best Picture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Picture "Best Picture") nomination at the [81st Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/81st_Academy_Awards "81st Academy Awards"). This decision received substantial criticism and was described as a "snub" by many publications.[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-125)[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-126)[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Sims-127) The backlash to the decision was such that, for the [82nd Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/82nd_Academy_Awards "82nd Academy Awards") awards in 2010, the Academy increased the limit for Best Picture nominees from five to ten, a change known as "The Dark Knight Rule".[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-128)[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-129)[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-130)[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Sims-127)

### Allegations of a lack of diversity

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Allegations of a lack of diversity")]

The Academy Awards have long received criticism over its lack of diversity among the nominees.[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-131)[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-132)[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-133) This criticism is based on the statistics from every Academy Awards since 1929, which show that only 6.4% of Academy Award nominees have been non-white and since 1991, 11.2% of nominees have been non-white, with the rate of winners being even more polarizing.[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-134) For a variety of reasons, including marketability and historical bans on interracial couples, a number of high-profile Oscars have been given to [yellowface](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Examples_of_yellowface "Examples of yellowface") portrayals, as well as performances of Asian characters rewritten for white characters.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-135)[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-136) It took until 2023 for an Asian woman to win an [Academy Award for Best Actress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Actress "Academy Award for Best Actress"), when [Michelle Yeoh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Yeoh "Michelle Yeoh") received the award for her performance in *[Everything Everywhere All at Once](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everything_Everywhere_All_at_Once "Everything Everywhere All at Once")*. The [88th awards ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/88th_Academy_Awards "88th Academy Awards") became the target of a boycott, popularized on social media with the [hashtag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashtag "Hashtag") #OscarsSoWhite, based on activists' perception that its all-white acting nominee list reflected bias.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-137) In response, the Academy initiated "historic" changes in membership by 2020.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-138)[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-139) Some media critics claim the Academy's efforts to address its purported racial, gender and national biases are merely distractions.[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-140)[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-141)[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-142)[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-143) By contrast, the [Golden Globe Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Awards "Golden Globe Awards") already have multiple winners of Asian descent in leading actress categories.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-144) Some question whether the Academy's definition of "merit" is just or empowering for non-Americans.[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-145)

The Academy's Representation and Inclusion Standards have been criticized for excluding [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews "Jews") as a distinct underrepresented class.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-146)

### Miscategorization of actors

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Miscategorization of actors")]

See also: [Carol (film) § Oscar category](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carol_\(film\)#Oscar_category "Carol (film)")

The Academy has no rules for how to categorize whether a performance is leading or supporting, and it is up to the discretion of the studios whether a given performance is submitted for either Best Actor/Actress or Best Supporting Actor/Actress. This has led to situations where a film has two or more co-leads, and one of these is submitted in a supporting category to avoid the two leads competing against each other, and to increase the film's chances of winning. This practice has been derisively called "category fraud".[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-liveabout_com-147)[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-polygon_com-148)

For example, [Rooney Mara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rooney_Mara "Rooney Mara") was nominated for Best Supporting Actress for *[Carol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carol_\(film\) "Carol (film)")* (2015), despite her having a comparable amount of screentime to [Cate Blanchett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cate_Blanchett "Cate Blanchett"), who was nominated for Best Actress. Another example is *[Once Upon a Time in Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Once_Upon_a_Time_in_Hollywood "Once Upon a Time in Hollywood")* (2019), where [Brad Pitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Pitt "Brad Pitt") was nominated for and won Best Supporting Actor, even though he played an equally important role to Best Actor nominee [Leonardo DiCaprio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_DiCaprio "Leonardo DiCaprio"). In both these cases, critics argued that [The Weinstein Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weinstein_Company "The Weinstein Company"), the studio behind the former film, had placed someone who was actually a leading actor or actress into the supporting categories to avoid them competing against their co-lead.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-liveabout_com-147)[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-polygon_com-148)

### Symbolism or sentimentalization

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=30 "Edit section: Symbolism or sentimentalization")]

Acting prizes in certain years have been criticized for not recognizing superior performances so much as being awarded for personal popularity,[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-149) to make up for a "snub" for a work that proved in time to be more popular or renowned than the one awarded, or presented as a "career honor" to recognize a distinguished nominee's entire body of work.[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-levy-150)

### Recognition of streaming media film

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=31 "Edit section: Recognition of streaming media film")]

Following the [91st Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/91st_Academy_Awards "91st Academy Awards") in February 2019 in which the [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix")-broadcast film *[Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roma_\(2018_film\) "Roma (2018 film)")* had been nominated for ten awards including the Best Picture category, Steven Spielberg and other members of the Academy discussed changing the requirements through the Board of Governors for films as to exclude those from Netflix and other media streaming services. Spielberg had been concerned that Netflix as a movie production and distribution studio could spend much more than for typical Oscar-winning films and have much wider and earlier distribution than for other Best Picture-nominated films, while still being able to meet the minimal theatrical-run status to qualify for an Oscar.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-151)

The [United States Department of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Justice "United States Department of Justice"), having heard of this potential rule change, wrote a letter to the Academy in March 2019, cautioning them that placing additional restrictions on films that originate from streaming media services without proper justification could raise anti-trust concerns against the Academy.[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-152) Following its April 2019 board meeting, the Academy Board of Governors agreed to retain the current rules that allow for streaming media films to be eligible for Oscars as long as they enjoy limited theatrical runs.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-153)

### 2022 Chris Rock and Will Smith slapping incident

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=32 "Edit section: 2022 Chris Rock and Will Smith slapping incident")]

Main article: [Chris Rock–Will Smith slapping incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Rock%E2%80%93Will_Smith_slapping_incident "Chris Rock–Will Smith slapping incident")

During the [94th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/94th_Academy_Awards "94th Academy Awards") on March 27, 2022, [Chris Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Rock "Chris Rock") joked about [Jada Pinkett Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jada_Pinkett_Smith "Jada Pinkett Smith")'s shaved head[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Alopecia-154) with a *[G.I. Jane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G.I._Jane "G.I. Jane")* reference. [Will Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_Smith "Will Smith") walked onstage and slapped Rock across the face, then returned to his seat and told Rock, twice, to "Keep my wife's name out \[of] your fucking mouth!"[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-155)[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-156)[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-157) While later accepting the [Best Actor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Actor "Academy Award for Best Actor") award for *[King Richard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Richard_\(film\) "King Richard (film)")*, Smith apologized to the Academy and the other nominees, but not to Rock.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-158)[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-159)[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-160) Rock decided not to press charges against Smith.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-161)

On April 8, 2022, the Academy made an announcement via a letter sent by president [David Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Rubin_\(casting_director\) "David Rubin (casting director)") and CEO [Dawn Hudson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawn_Hudson "Dawn Hudson") informing the public that Will Smith had received a ten-year ban from attending the Oscars as a result of the incident.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-162)

### Refusals of the award

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=33 "Edit section: Refusals of the award")]

Some winners critical of the Academy Awards have boycotted the ceremonies and refused to accept their Oscars. The first to do so was screenwriter [Dudley Nichols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dudley_Nichols "Dudley Nichols") (Best Writing in 1935 for *[The Informer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Informer_\(1935_film\) "The Informer (1935 film)")*). Nichols boycotted the [8th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8th_Academy_Awards "8th Academy Awards") ceremony because of conflicts between the Academy and the Writers' Guild.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DYK-163) Nichols eventually accepted the 1935 award three years later, at the 1938 ceremony. Nichols was nominated for three further Academy Awards during his career.

[George C. Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_C._Scott "George C. Scott") became the second person to refuse his award (Best Actor in 1970 for *[Patton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patton_\(film\) "Patton (film)")*) at the [43rd Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/43rd_Academy_Awards "43rd Academy Awards") ceremony. Scott described it as a "meat parade", saying, "I don't want any part of it".[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-164)[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-165)[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-166)

The third person to refuse the award was [Marlon Brando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marlon_Brando "Marlon Brando"), who refused his award (Best Actor for 1972's *[The Godfather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Godfather "The Godfather")*), citing the film industry's discrimination against and mistreatment of [Native Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas "Indigenous peoples of the Americas"). At the [45th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/45th_Academy_Awards "45th Academy Awards") ceremony, Brando asked actress and [civil rights activist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_rights_activist "Civil rights activist") [Sacheen Littlefeather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacheen_Littlefeather "Sacheen Littlefeather") to read a 15-page speech in his place, detailing his criticisms, for which there was [booing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booing "Booing") and [cheering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheering "Cheering") by the audience.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-167)[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DYK-163) In 2022, Littlefeather was accused by her sisters of misrepresenting her ancestry as Native American.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-168)[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-169)[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-170)[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-171)[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-172)

### Disqualifications

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=34 "Edit section: Disqualifications")]

Seven films have had nominations revoked before the official award ceremony:[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-173)

- *[The Circus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Circus_\(1928_film\) "The Circus (1928 film)")* (1928) – The film was voluntarily removed by the Academy from competitive categories, to award [Charlie Chaplin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Chaplin "Charlie Chaplin") a special award.
- *[Hondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hondo_\(film\) "Hondo (film)")* (1953) – Removed from the Best Story ballot after letters from the producer and nominee questioned its inclusion in the category.
- *[High Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Society_\(1955_film\) "High Society (1955 film)")* (1955) – Withdrawn from screenwriting ballot after being mistaken for the [1956 movie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Society_\(1956_film\) "High Society (1956 film)") of the same title.
- *[The Godfather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Godfather "The Godfather")* (1972) – Initially nominated for eleven awards, its nomination for Best Original Score was revoked after it was discovered that its main theme was very similar to music that [the score's composer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nino_Rota "Nino Rota") had written for an earlier film. None of its other nominations were revoked, and it received three Oscars, including Best Picture.
- *[A Place in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Place_in_the_World_\(film\) "A Place in the World (film)")* (1992) – Removed from the Best Foreign Language Film ballot after it was discovered that the country which submitted the film exercised insufficient artistic control.
- *[Alone Yet Not Alone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alone_Yet_Not_Alone "Alone Yet Not Alone")* (2014) – The film's title song, "Alone Yet Not Alone", was removed from the Best Original Song ballot after [Bruce Broughton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Broughton "Bruce Broughton") was found to have improperly contacted other members of the Academy's musical branch; this was the first time that a film was removed from a ballot for ethical reasons.
- *[13 Hours: The Secret Soldiers of Benghazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13_Hours:_The_Secret_Soldiers_of_Benghazi "13 Hours: The Secret Soldiers of Benghazi")* (2017) – Sound mixer [Greg P. Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_P._Russell "Greg P. Russell")'s nomination was rescinded one day before the Awards when it was discovered he had improperly contacted voters by telephone. In this case, the nominations for the other three nominated sound mixers, [Gary Summers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Summers "Gary Summers"), [Jeffrey J. Haboush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_J._Haboush "Jeffrey J. Haboush") and [Mac Ruth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mac_Ruth "Mac Ruth"), were allowed to stand.

One film was disqualified after winning the award, and had the winner return the Oscar:

- *[Young Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Americans_\(1967_film\) "Young Americans (1967 film)")* (1969) – Initially won the award for Best Documentary Feature, but was later revoked after it was revealed that it had opened theatrically prior to the eligibility period.

One film had its nomination revoked after the award ceremony when it had not won the Oscar:

- *[Tuba Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuba_Atlantic "Tuba Atlantic")* (2011) – Its nomination for Best Live Action Short Film was revoked when it was discovered that the film had aired on television in 2010, before its theatrical release.

### Remarks about animated films as children's genre

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Remarks about animated films as children's genre")]

At the [94th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/94th_Academy_Awards "94th Academy Awards") in 2022, the award for the Best Animated Feature was presented by three actresses who portrayed Disney princess characters in [live-action remakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Disney_live-action_adaptations_and_remakes_of_Disney_animated_films "List of Disney live-action adaptations and remakes of Disney animated films") of their respective animated films: [Lily James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lily_James "Lily James") (*[Cinderella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinderella_\(2015_American_film\) "Cinderella (2015 American film)")*), [Naomi Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Scott "Naomi Scott") (*[Aladdin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aladdin_\(2019_film\) "Aladdin (2019 film)")*), and [Halle Bailey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halle_Bailey "Halle Bailey") (*[The Little Mermaid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Little_Mermaid_\(2023_film\) "The Little Mermaid (2023 film)")*). While introducing the category, Bailey stated that animated films are "formative experiences as kids who watch them," as James put it, "So many kids watch these movies over and over, over and over again." Scott added: "I see some parents who know exactly what we're talking about."[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-174) The remarks were heavily criticized by animation enthusiasts and those working in the industry as infantilizing the medium and perpetuating the stigma that animated works are [strictly for children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children%27s_film "Children's film"), especially since the industry was credited with sustaining the flow of [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_the_United_States "Cinema of the United States") content and revenue during the height of the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic"). [Phil Lord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Lord_and_Christopher_Miller "Phil Lord and Christopher Miller"), co-producer of one of the nominated films, *[The Mitchells vs. the Machines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mitchells_vs._the_Machines "The Mitchells vs. the Machines")*, tweeted that it was "super cool to position animation as something that kids watch and adults have to endure." The film's official social media accounts responded to the joke with an image reading: "Animation is cinema."[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175)[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-176) A week later, Lord and his producing partner [Christopher Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Lord_and_Christopher_Miller "Phil Lord and Christopher Miller") wrote a guest column in *[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_\(magazine\) "Variety (magazine)")* criticizing the Academy for the joke and how Hollywood has treated animation, writing that "no one set out to diminish animated films, but it's high time we set out to elevate them." They also suggested to the Academy that the category should be presented by a filmmaker who respects the art of animation as cinema.[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177)

Adding to the controversy was that the award for [Best Animated Short Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Animated_Short_Film "Academy Award for Best Animated Short Film") (the nominees for which were mostly made up of shorts not aimed at children) was one of the [eight categories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/94th_Academy_Awards#Effort_to_shorten_the_ceremony "94th Academy Awards") that were not presented during the live broadcast.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-178) The winner for the Best Animated Short award was *[The Windshield Wiper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Windshield_Wiper "The Windshield Wiper")*, a multilingual Spanish-American film which is [adult animated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_animation "Adult animation"), while another nominee in three categories: Best Animated Feature, [Best Documentary Feature Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Documentary_Feature_Film "Academy Award for Best Documentary Feature Film"), and [Best International Feature Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_International_Feature_Film "Academy Award for Best International Feature Film"), was *[Flee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flee_\(film\) "Flee (film)")*, a PG-13 rated animated documentary about an Afghan refugee. [Alberto Mielgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Mielgo "Alberto Mielgo"), director of *The Windshield Wiper*, later gave an acceptance speech for the Oscar: "Animation is an art that includes every single art that you can imagine. Animation for adults is a fact. It's happening. Let's call it cinema. I'm very honored because this is just the beginning of what we can do with animation."[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-179) Some speculations suggested that the speech played a role in the decision not to broadcast the award.[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180)

Another factor is that numerous animated films have been made for mature audiences or with ranges of PG-13 or more, with a few of them—*[The Triplets of Belleville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Triplets_of_Belleville "The Triplets of Belleville")*, *[Persepolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persepolis_\(film\) "Persepolis (film)")*, *[Chico and Rita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chico_and_Rita "Chico and Rita")*, *[The Wind Rises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wind_Rises "The Wind Rises")*, *[Anomalisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anomalisa "Anomalisa")*, *[My Life as a Courgette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Life_as_a_Courgette "My Life as a Courgette")*, *[The Breadwinner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Breadwinner_\(film\) "The Breadwinner (film)")*, *[Loving Vincent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loving_Vincent "Loving Vincent")*, *[Isle of Dogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle_of_Dogs_\(film\) "Isle of Dogs (film)")*, *[I Lost My Body](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Lost_My_Body "I Lost My Body")*, and *[Flee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flee_\(film\) "Flee (film)")*—having been nominated in this category, with *[The Boy and the Heron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Boy_and_the_Heron "The Boy and the Heron")* being the first adult animated film (in this case, PG-13-rated) to win in the [96th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/96th_Academy_Awards "96th Academy Awards").[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-181)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-182)

These comments came as *#NewDeal4Animation*, a movement of animation workers demanding equal pay, treatment and recognition alongside their contemporaries working in live-action, was picking up momentum during negotiations for a new contract between [The Animation Guild, IATSE Local 839](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Animation_Guild,_IATSE_Local_839 "The Animation Guild, IATSE Local 839")/[SAG-AFTRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAG-AFTRA "SAG-AFTRA") and the [Alliance of Motion Picture and Television Producers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_Motion_Picture_and_Television_Producers "Alliance of Motion Picture and Television Producers"),[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183) and the presentation is being used to rally the movement.

During the [96th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/96th_Academy_Awards "96th Academy Awards") in 2024, host [Jimmy Kimmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Kimmel "Jimmy Kimmel") said, "Please raise your hand if you let your kid fill out this part of the ballot." These remarks would again prompt backlash, with [Christopher Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Lord_and_Christopher_Miller "Phil Lord and Christopher Miller"), producer of that year's nominated *[Spider-Man: Across the Spider-Verse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spider-Man:_Across_the_Spider-Verse "Spider-Man: Across the Spider-Verse")*, tweeting out that the joke was "tired and lazy".[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184) The PG-13-rated *The Boy and the Heron* would subsequently win the award.

## Associated events

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=36 "Edit section: Associated events")]

The following events are closely associated with the annual Academy Awards:

- [Governors Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governors_Awards "Governors Awards"), which include the presentation of the [Academy Honorary Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Honorary_Award "Academy Honorary Award"), the [Jean Hersholt Humanitarian Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Hersholt_Humanitarian_Award "Jean Hersholt Humanitarian Award"), and the [Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving_G._Thalberg_Memorial_Award "Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award")[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Governors_Awards-185)
- The 25th [Independent Spirit Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Spirit_Awards "Independent Spirit Awards") (2010), usually held in [Santa Monica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica,_California "Santa Monica, California"), California the Saturday before the Oscars, marked the first time it was moved to a Friday and a change of venue to [L.A. Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L.A._Live "L.A. Live")
- The annual "Night Before", traditionally held at [the Beverly Hills Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beverly_Hills_Hotel "The Beverly Hills Hotel"), begun in 2002 and generally known as *the&#x20;*&#x70;arty of the season, benefits the [Motion Picture & Television Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_Picture_%26_Television_Fund "Motion Picture & Television Fund"), which operates a retirement home for SAG actors in the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley "San Fernando Valley")
- [Elton John AIDS Foundation Academy Award Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elton_John_AIDS_Foundation_Academy_Award_Party "Elton John AIDS Foundation Academy Award Party") airs the awards live at the nearby [Pacific Design Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Design_Center "Pacific Design Center")[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-186)
- The Governors Ball is the Academy's official after-party, including dinner (until 2011), and is adjacent to the awards-presentation venue.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-187)
- The *[Vanity Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_Fair_\(magazine\) "Vanity Fair (magazine)")* after-party, historically at the former Morton's restaurant, has been at the [Sunset Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset_Tower "Sunset Tower") since 2009.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-hollywoodreporter.com-188)

## Presenter and performer gifts

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=37 "Edit section: Presenter and performer gifts")]

It has become a tradition to give out gift bags to the presenters and performers at the Oscars. In recent years, these gifts have been extended to award nominees and winners.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ABCGiftBag-189) The value of each of these gift bags can reach into the tens of thousands of dollars. In 2014, the value was reported to be as high as $80,000.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CBSGiftBag-190) The value has risen to the point where the U.S. [Internal Revenue Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Revenue_Service "Internal Revenue Service") issued a statement regarding the gifts and their taxable status.[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-IRSGiftBag-191)

Oscar gift bags have included vacation packages to Hawaii and Mexico and Japan, a private dinner party for the recipient and friends at a restaurant, [videophones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Videotelephony "Videotelephony"), a four-night stay at a hotel, watches, bracelets, spa treatments, bottles of vodka, maple salad dressing, weight-loss gummie candy and up to $25,000 worth of cosmetic treatments and rejuvenation procedures such as lip fillers and chemical peels from New York City facial plastic surgeon Konstantin Vasyukevich.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ABCGiftBag-189)[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ABCGiftBag2-192)[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-VarietyGiftBag-193)[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ForbesGiftBag2020-194)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-YahooGiftBag2020-195) Some of the gifts have even had a "risque" element to them; in 2014, the adult products retailer [Adam & Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_%26_Eve_\(company\) "Adam & Eve (company)") had a "Secret Room Gifting Suite". Celebrities visiting the gifting suite included [Judith Hoag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Hoag "Judith Hoag"), [Carolyn Hennesy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolyn_Hennesy "Carolyn Hennesy"), [Kate Linder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Linder "Kate Linder"), [Chris Mulkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Mulkey "Chris Mulkey"), [Jim O'Heir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_O%27Heir "Jim O'Heir") and [John Salley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Salley "John Salley").[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AandEOscarGifts-196)

## Footnotes

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Academy_Awards\&action=edit\&section=44 "Edit section: Footnotes")]

1. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-32)** Sources conflict on whether she actually said this. *Deadline* puts doubt on it, saying "'He reminds me of my Uncle Oscar,' she was reported to have said, while in the hearing of a 'nearby newspaper columnist' who picked up the anecdote and ran with it the next day". *Variety* and *The Hollywood Reporter* state with certainty that she made a claim to the Oscar nickname.
2. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-60)** Starting with the [2017 awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/89th_Academy_Awards "89th Academy Awards"), a qualifying release for the Documentary Feature award can take place anywhere in the [five boroughs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boroughs_of_New_York_City "Boroughs of New York City") of New York City. Previously, a New York City qualifying run could only take place in [Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan "Manhattan"). Since then, [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn "Brooklyn") has also become a popular location.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-59)
